there exists
\mu^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}{A}
such that
\mu\,[\kappa]=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inl})(\mu^{\prime})
,
there is a
\nu^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime}
, and there is a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
.

(2)

there exists
\mu^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu\,[\kappa]=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mu^{\prime})
and
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu\,[\kappa]))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
.

(3)

there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
,
p\in(0,1)
such that
\mu\,[\kappa]={\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inl})(\mu_{1})}\oplus_{p}{\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mu_{2})}
,
there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inl})(\mu_{1}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mu_{2}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}

We first show that
S^{\kappa}(\mu,\nu)
implies
{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}(\mu\,[\kappa],\nu)
. Cases (1) in the two definitions are equal. In the case of (2) of
S^{\kappa}(\mu,\nu)
, to prove (2) of

{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}(\mu\,[\kappa],\nu)
, consider
\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}\circ\mathsf{ev}_{\kappa})(\mu^{\prime}):\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, where
\mathsf{ev}_{\kappa}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{A}\to\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A}

is evaluation at
\kappa
.
Since
\mu=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mu^{\prime})
, it is easy to see that
\mu\,[\kappa]=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}\circ\mathsf{ev}_{\kappa})(\mu^{\prime}))
,
and clearly
\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu\,[\kappa])=\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}(\mathsf{run}(\mu)\,[\kappa])
so that
\triangleright^{\kappa}(S^{\kappa}(\mathsf{run}(\mu),\nu))
implies
\triangleright^{\kappa}(\alpha:\kappa)(S^{\kappa}(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu\,[\kappa])\,[\alpha],\nu))
,
which implies
\triangleright^{\kappa}(\alpha:\kappa)(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu\,[\kappa])\,[\alpha]{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
by guarded recursion as desired. The case of (3) is similar.

In the other direction we just show that (2) of
(\mu\,[\kappa]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
implies (2) of
S^{\kappa}(\mu,\nu)
. So suppose the former, and apply
the equivalence of Theorem
3.6
to
\mu
. Since
\mu\,[\kappa]
is in the second of the three possible cases, so must
\mu
be. In other words,
there must exist a
\mu^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{A})
such that
\mu=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mu^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}\circ\mathsf{ev}_{\kappa})(\mu^{\prime\prime})=\mu^{\prime}
.
Then, as before,
\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu\,[\kappa])=\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}(\mathsf{run}(\mu)\,[\kappa])
and so the assumption
\triangleright^{\kappa}(\alpha:\kappa)(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu\,[\kappa])\,[\alpha]{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)

is equivalent to
\triangleright^{\kappa}(\alpha:\kappa)(\mathsf{run}(\mu)\,[\kappa]{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
, which by guarded recursion implies
\triangleright^{\kappa}(S^{\kappa}(\mathsf{run}(\mu),\nu))

as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

Recall that, by definition of
\leq_{\mathrm{lim}}
, we have to prove that for all
n
and
\varepsilon>0
there exists
m
such that
\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\mu)\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon
.
We first show that the lemma holds whenever
\mu:\mathcal{D}{1}
. Here, there exists
\nu^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}{1}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime}
and there is a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime})
. Then for any
p\in(0,1)
there exist
\nu^{\prime\prime}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1}
such that
\nu\leadsto{\nu^{\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu^{\prime\prime}}
. We now have:

•

\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\mu)=\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\nu^{\prime})=1

•

By Lemma
4.20
,
\mathsf{PT}_{(.)}(\nu)=_{\mathrm{lim}}\mathsf{PT}_{(.)}(\nu^{\prime})
, and there exists
m
such that
\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\nu^{\prime})\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon
.

Otherwise, we do induction on
n
. For
n=0
,
\varepsilon>0
, we have two cases for
\mu{}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu
:

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1})
, then
\mathsf{PT}_{0}(\mu)=0
and the statement is trivially true.

•

If there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{1},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1}),p\in(0,1)
such that
\mu={\mu_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\mu_{2}}
, and there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu_{1}
,
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu_{2}
:

–

\mathsf{PT}_{0}(\mu)=p

–

By the previous case,
\mathsf{PT}_{(.)}(\mu_{1})\leq\mathsf{PT}_{(.)}(\nu_{1})
, and furthermore,
\mathsf{PT}_{0}(\mu_{1})=1
.
Therefore, there exists
m^{\prime}
such that
1\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}(\nu_{1})+\varepsilon/2
, and thus

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\leq p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}(\nu_{1})+p\cdot\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle\leq p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}(\nu_{1})+p\cdot\varepsilon/2+(1-p)\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}(\nu_{2})+(1-p)\cdot\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle=\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})+\varepsilon/2

so
p\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})+\varepsilon/2
.
By Lemma
4.20
,
\mathsf{PT}_{(.)}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})=_{\mathrm{lim}}\mathsf{PT}_{(.)}(\nu)
, so there exists
m
such that

\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon/2
,
and then we can conclude
\mathsf{PT}_{0}(\mu)\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon
.

For
n=n^{\prime}+1
,
\varepsilon>0
, we have again two cases for
\mu{}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu
.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1})
and
\mathsf{run}(\mu){}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu
then
\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\mu)=\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mathsf{run}(\mu))
. By the induction hypothesis for
n^{\prime}
, we know that then there is an
m
such that
\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mathsf{run}(\mu))\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon
, and hence also
\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\mu)\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon
.

•

If there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{1},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1}),p\in(0,1)
such that
\mu={\mu_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\mu_{2}}
, and there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{1}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu_{1}
,
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu_{2}
:
Then:

–

\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\mu)=p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mu_{1})+(1-p)\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mathsf{run}(\mu_{2}))

–

By definition of the lifting,
\mathsf{run}(\mu_{2}){}\,{}\overline{\mathsf{eq}_{1}}\,{}\nu_{2}
. By the inductive hypothesis, there exist
m_{1},m_{2}
such that

\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mu_{1})=1\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m_{1}}(\nu_{1})+\varepsilon/2
and

\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mathsf{run}(\mu_{2}))\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m_{2}}(\nu_{2})+\varepsilon/2
.

–

Let
m^{\prime}
be the largest of
m_{1},m_{2}
. By Lemma
4.20
there exists
m
such that

\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}({\nu_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\nu_{2}})\leq\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon/2
.

Putting all of the above together, we now have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{PT}_{n}(\mu)

\displaystyle=p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mu_{1})+(1-p)\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{n^{\prime}}(\mathsf{run}(\mu_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq p\cdot(\mathsf{PT}_{m_{1}}(\nu_{1})+\varepsilon/2)+(1-p)\cdot(\mathsf{PT}_{m_{2}}(\nu_{2})+\varepsilon/2)

\displaystyle\leq p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}(\nu_{1})+(1-p)\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m^{\prime}}(\nu_{2})+\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle\leq p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon/2+\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle=p\cdot\mathsf{PT}_{m}(\nu)+\varepsilon

which is what we needed to show. ∎

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

For left to right, suppose that
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
. We need to show that also
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
. We consider the three possibilities of Definition
7.2
.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{A}
, then we need to show that there is a
\nu^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{D}{B}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime\prime})
. From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
we get a
\nu^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{D}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime\prime})
.

We just need to show that also
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
. For any
p\in(0,1)
, we know from
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, that there is a
\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}}
.

By Lemma
4.10
.
3
, there is an
n
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu
. By Lemma
4.10
.
2
we know:

\displaystyle\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu

\displaystyle\leadsto\mathsf{run}^{n}({\nu^{\prime\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle={\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu^{\prime\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle={\nu^{\prime\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}},

where the last equality holds because
\nu^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{D}{B}
.
Hence by transitivity of
\leadsto
, also:

\nu^{\prime}\leadsto{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}},

proving that indeed
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
, then we need to show that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime})
. From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
we know that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
, and so by guarded recursion, also
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime})
.

•

If there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, and
p\in(0,1)
such that
\mu={\mu_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\mu_{2}}
, then we need to show that there exist
\nu^{\prime}_{1},\nu^{\prime}_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu^{\prime}_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu^{\prime}_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}_{2}
.

We know from
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
that there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
.

By Lemma
4.10
.
3
, there is an
n
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu
. Then by Lemma
4.10
.
2
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu

\displaystyle\leadsto\mathsf{run}^{n}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle={\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{2}}

Then by Lemma
4.19
.
1
also
\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{2}}
, and finally by Lemma
4.19
.
2

\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{2}}
.

What is left to show is that
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{2}
. Since by Lemma
4.10
.
1

\nu_{1}\leadsto\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}\leadsto\mathsf{run}^{n}\nu_{2}
, and
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, these claims follow from the first two cases of this lemma.

For right to left, suppose that
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
. We need to show that also
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
. We consider the three possibilities of Definition
7.2
.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{A}
, then we need to show that there is a
\nu^{\prime\prime}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime\prime})
. From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
we get a
\nu^{\prime\prime}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime\prime})
. By Lemma
4.19
,
\nu\leadsto\nu^{\prime}
implies that also
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and so indeed
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
, then we need to show that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
. From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
we know that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime})
, and so by guarded recursion, also
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
.

•

If there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, and
p\in(0,1)
such that
\mu={\mu_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\mu_{2}}
, then we need to show that there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
.

From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
we know that there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
. By Lemma
4.19
,
\nu\leadsto\nu^{\prime}
implies that also
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and so indeed
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
. ∎

Proof of Lemma
7.6
.

Given
\mu:\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A},\nu:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
, suppose that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime}
and
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
.
We consider three cases, one for each part of Definition
7.2
:

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{A}
, then we need to show that there is a
\nu^{\prime\prime}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime\prime})
. From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
we get a
\nu^{\prime\prime}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, and a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu^{\prime\prime})
. By transitivity of
\leadsto^{\approx}
(Lemma
4.17
), also
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, so this case is immediate from the assumptions.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
, then we need to show that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
. From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
we know that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime})
, and so by guarded recursion, also
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
.

•

If there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, and
p\in(0,1)
such that
\mu={\mu_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\mu_{2}}
, then we need to show that there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
.

From our assumption
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
we know that there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, and
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
. By transitivity of
\leadsto^{\approx}
(Lemma
4.17
), also
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu^{\prime\prime}
, so this case is again immediate from the assumptions. ∎

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

For the first statement, notice that

{(\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}\mu_{1})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}\mu_{2})}=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})({\delta(\mu_{1})}\oplus_{p}{\delta(\mu_{2})}),

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}

\displaystyle(\char 12\relax{\alpha}{\kappa}{(\mu_{1}\,[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mu_{2}\,[\alpha])})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\delta(\char 12\relax{\alpha}{\kappa}{(\mu_{1}\,[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mu_{2}\,[\alpha])})),

where both right hands of these equations are of form
\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{inr})(\mu^{\prime})
with
\mu^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
. Therefore, by Definition
7.2
(
2
) we have:

\displaystyle{(\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}\mu_{1})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}\mu_{2})}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\;

\displaystyle{\triangleright}\,(\alpha\!:\!\kappa).{(((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}{(\delta(\mu_{1}))}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\delta(\mu_{2}))}))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle{\triangleright}\,(\alpha\!:\!\kappa).{(({(\mu_{1}\,[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mu_{2}\,[\alpha])}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle{\triangleright}\,(\alpha\!:\!\kappa).{(((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\delta(\char 12\relax{\beta}{\kappa}{(\mu_{1}\,[\beta])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mu_{2}\,[\beta])})))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\;

\displaystyle\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\char 12\relax{\beta}{\kappa}{(\mu_{1}\,[\beta])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{(\mu_{2}\,[\beta])}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu.

The second statement follows from Lemma
7.5

since both

\displaystyle{(\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}(\nu_{1}))}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{(\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}(\nu_{2}))}

\displaystyle\leadsto{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle\leadsto{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}.\qed

Proof of Lemma
7.8
.

We analyse the 3*3 different possibilities for
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
.

(1)

If both
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}{A}
, then from
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
we know that there are
\nu_{1}^{\prime},\nu_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}{B}
such that
\nu_{1}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu_{1}^{\prime}
and
\nu_{2}\leadsto^{\approx}\nu_{2}^{\prime}
, and that there are
\rho_{1}:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu_{1}^{\prime})
and
\rho_{2}:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu_{2}^{\prime})
.

By Lemma
4.17
we have
{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}^{\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}^{\prime}}
. Also:
{\rho_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\rho_{2}}:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}},{\nu_{1}^{\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}^{\prime}})
, and so
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
via Definition
7.2
.
1
.

(2)

If
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
, then
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
follows immediately from the given assumptions, via Definition
7.2
.
3
.

(3)

If
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
, then again
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
follows immediately from the given assumptions, via Definition
7.2
.
3
, and using that
{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}=({\mu_{2}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{1-p}{\mu_{1}})
.

(4)

If
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and for
\mu_{2}
there exist
\mu_{21}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{22}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu_{2}={\mu_{21}}\oplus_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{22}}
, then:

\displaystyle{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{({\mu_{21}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{22}})}

\displaystyle={({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{p}{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\mu_{21}})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}{\mu_{22}},

where
{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{p}{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\mu_{21}}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
\mu_{22}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
.

So by Definition
7.2
.
3
, we need to show that there exist
\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu^{\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}{\nu^{\prime\prime}}
, and
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{p}{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\mu_{21}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
and
\mu_{22}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime\prime}
.

We know from
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
that there exist
\nu_{21},\nu_{22}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu_{2}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{21}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p^{\prime}}{\nu_{22}}
, and
\mu_{21}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{21}
and
\mu_{22}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{22}
.
We also know that
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
by assumption.
We then know, using the reflexivity and sum properties of Lemma
4.17
, that:

\displaystyle{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{({\nu_{21}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p^{\prime}}{\nu_{22}})}

\displaystyle={({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{p}{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\nu_{21}})}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}{\nu_{22}}.

So we choose:

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{p}{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\nu_{21}}

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\nu_{22}.

Then from the first item of this lemma we get that
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{p}{p+(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\mu_{21}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
, and finally it follows directly from our assumptions that
\mu_{22}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime\prime}
.

(5)

If
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and for
\mu_{1}
there exist
\mu_{11}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{12}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu_{1}={\mu_{11}}\oplus_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{12}}
, then:

\displaystyle{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={({\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{12}})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={({\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{pp^{\prime}}{1-(1-p^{\prime})p}}{\mu_{2}})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{1-(1-p^{\prime})p}{\mu_{12}},

where
{\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{pp^{\prime}}{1-(1-p^{\prime})p}}{\mu_{2}}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
\mu_{12}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
.

We again need to satisfy Definition
7.2
.
3
, and via similar arguments as the previous case we can choose

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{pp^{\prime}}{1-(1-p^{\prime})p}}{\nu_{2}}

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\nu_{12}

to do so.

(6)

If both
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
then we know that

\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu_{1}))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1})

and

\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu_{2}))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}).

Then by guarded recursion we know:

\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ ({((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu_{1}))[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu_{2}))[\alpha])}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})),

which is the same as:

\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})),

which shows that
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
via Definition
7.2
.
2
.

(7)

If
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
and for
\mu_{1}
there exist
\mu_{11}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{12}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu_{1}={\mu_{11}}\oplus_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{12}}
, then:

\displaystyle{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={({\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{12}})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{pp^{\prime}}{({\mu_{12}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{(1-p^{\prime})p}{1-pp^{\prime}}}{\mu_{2}})},

where
\mu_{11}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
{\mu_{12}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{(1-p^{\prime})p}{1-pp^{\prime}}}{\mu_{2}}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
.

We know from
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
that there exist
\nu_{11},\nu_{12}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu_{1}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p_{1}}{\nu_{12}}
, and
\mu_{11}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{11}
and
\mu_{12}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{12}
.

If we pick

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\nu_{11}

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle={\nu_{12}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{(1-p^{\prime})p}{1-pp^{\prime}}}{\nu_{2}},

then by the reflexivity and sum properties of Lemma
4.17

\displaystyle{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leadsto^{\approx}{({\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p^{\prime}}{\nu_{12}})}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}

\displaystyle={\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{pp^{\prime}}{({\nu_{12}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{(1-p^{\prime})p}{1-pp^{\prime}}}{\nu_{2}})}

\displaystyle={\nu^{\prime}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{pp^{\prime}}{\nu^{\prime\prime}}

In addition,
\mu_{11}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{11}
immediately gives
\mu_{11}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
, and from the sixth item of this lemma we know that
\mu_{12}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{12}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
imply
({\mu_{12}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{(1-p^{\prime})p}{1-pp^{\prime}}}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{12}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{(1-p^{\prime})p}{1-pp^{\prime}}}{\nu_{2}})=\nu^{\prime\prime}
.

Hence we have
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
via Definition
7.2
.
3
.

(8)

If
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})}
and for
\mu_{2}
there exist
\mu_{21}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{22}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu_{2}={\mu_{21}}\oplus_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{22}}
, then:

\displaystyle{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{({\mu_{21}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p^{\prime}}{\mu_{22}})}

\displaystyle={\mu_{21}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{(1-p)p^{\prime}}{({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{p}{1-(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\mu_{22}})},

where
\mu_{21}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{p}{1-(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\mu_{22}}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
.

We again need to satisfy Definition
7.2
.
3
, and via similar arguments as the previous case we can choose

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\nu_{21}

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle={\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{p}{1-(1-p)p^{\prime}}}{\nu_{12}}

to do so.

(9)

Finally, if for
\mu_{1}
there exist
\mu_{11}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{12}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu_{1}={\mu_{11}}\oplus_{p_{1}}{\mu_{12}}
, and for
\mu_{2}
there exist
\mu_{21}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{22}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
such that
\mu_{2}={\mu_{21}}\oplus_{p_{2}}{\mu_{22}}
, then:

\displaystyle{\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle={({\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p_{1}}{\mu_{12}})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{({\mu_{21}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p_{2}}{\mu_{22}})}

\displaystyle={({\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{pp_{1}}{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}}{\mu_{21}})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}{({\mu_{12}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{(1-p_{1})p}{1-(pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2})}}{\mu_{22}})},

where
{\mu_{11}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{pp_{1}}{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}}{\mu_{21}}:\mathcal{D}{A}
and
{\mu_{12}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{\frac{(1-p_{1})p}{1-(pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2})}}{\mu_{22}}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
.

We know from
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
that there exist
\nu_{11},\nu_{12}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu_{1}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p_{1}}{\nu_{12}}
, and
\mu_{11}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{11}
and
\mu_{12}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{12}
,
and from
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
that there exist
\nu_{21},\nu_{22}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu_{2}\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{21}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p_{2}}{\nu_{22}}
, and
\mu_{21}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{21}
and
\mu_{22}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{22}
.

Similar to previous arguments, we can take:

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{pp_{1}}{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}}{\nu_{21}}

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle={\nu_{12}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{(1-p_{1})p}{1-(pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2})}}{\nu_{22}}

Since by the sum property of Lemma
4.17
, and our assumptions:

\displaystyle{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leadsto^{\approx}{({\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p_{1}}{\nu_{12}})}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{({\nu_{21}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p_{2}}{\nu_{22}})}

\displaystyle={({\nu_{11}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{pp_{1}}{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}}{\nu_{21}})}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}{({\nu_{12}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{\frac{(1-p_{1})p}{1-(pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2})}}{\nu_{22}})}.

Then from the first item of this lemma we know that
({\mu_{11}}\oplus_{\frac{pp_{1}}{pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2}}}{\mu_{21}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime}
and from the sixth item of this lemma we know
({\mu_{12}}\oplus_{\frac{(1-p_{1})p}{1-(pp_{1}+(1-p)p_{2})}}{\mu_{22}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu^{\prime\prime}
.

This shows that
({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
via Definition
7.2
.
3
.∎

Proof of Lemma
7.9
.

The first statement is by induction on the coupling
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu)
.

If
\rho=\delta(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{a},{b}$}\mathclose{}{)}})
for some
a:A
and
b:B
such that
a{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}b
, then
\overline{f}(\mu)=\overline{f}(\delta^{\kappa}a)=f(a)

and
\overline{g}(\nu^{\prime})=\overline{g}(\delta^{\forall\!}b)=g(b)
are related in
{}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}
by assumption.

If
\rho={\rho_{1}}\oplus_{p}{\rho_{2}}
, let
\mu_{i}\triangleq\mathcal{D}{(\mathsf{pr}_{1})}(\rho_{i})
and
\nu_{i}\triangleq\mathcal{D}{(\mathsf{pr}_{2})}(\rho_{i})
for
i=1,2
.
By induction
\overline{f}(\mu_{i}){}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu_{i})
and since

\displaystyle\overline{f}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\overline{f}({\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}})={\overline{f}(\mu_{1})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\overline{f}(\mu_{2})}

\displaystyle\overline{g}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\overline{g}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})={\overline{g}(\nu_{1})}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\overline{g}(\nu_{2})}

the case follows from Lemma
7.8
.

For the second statement, suppose that
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
. We consider each of the three cases.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}{A}
, then there exist
\nu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu_{1}
and there exists a
\rho:\mathsf{Cpl}_{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}(\mu,\nu_{1})
.
What we want is to show that
\overline{f}(\mu){}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu)
.

Notice that since the
\leadsto^{\approx}
relation is preserved by homomorphisms (Lemma
4.18
),
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}\nu_{1}
implies
\overline{g}(\nu)\leadsto^{\approx}\overline{g}(\nu_{1})
. By Lemma
7.6
it is therefore enough to show that
\overline{f}(\mu){}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu_{1})
, which follows directly from the first statement of this lemma.

•

If
\mu:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, then
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu)
. By guarded recursion then also:
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (\overline{f}(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu)[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu))
. Note that
\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ (\overline{f}(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\mu)[\alpha])=(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\mu))[\alpha]))
. We show this by induction on
\mu
:

If
\mu:\delta(\mu^{\prime})
, then:

\displaystyle\overline{f}(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\delta(\mu^{\prime}))[\alpha])

\displaystyle=\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime}\,[\alpha])

\displaystyle=(\char 12\relax{\beta}{\kappa}\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime}\,[\beta]))[\alpha]

\displaystyle=(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\delta(\char 12\relax{\beta}{\kappa}(\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime}\,[\beta])))))[\alpha]

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\delta(\mu^{\prime})))[\alpha].

If
\mu={\mu^{\prime}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
, then:

\displaystyle\overline{f}(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}{((\mu^{\prime})\,[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{((\mu^{\prime\prime})\,[\alpha])}))

\displaystyle=\overline{f}({((\zeta^{\kappa}{\mu}^{\prime})\,[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{((\zeta^{\kappa}{\mu}^{\prime\prime})\,[\alpha])})

\displaystyle={(\overline{f}(\zeta^{\kappa}{\mu}^{\prime})\,[\alpha])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{(\overline{f}(\zeta^{\kappa}{\mu}^{\prime\prime})\,[\alpha])}

\displaystyle={(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime})))\,[\alpha]}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime\prime})))\,[\alpha]}

\displaystyle=\char 12\relax{\beta}{\kappa}{((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime})))\,[\beta])}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime\prime})))\,[\beta])}[\alpha]

\displaystyle=(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}{\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime})}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{\overline{f}(\mu^{\prime\prime})}))[\alpha]

\displaystyle=(\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}({\mu^{\prime}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{q}{\mu^{\prime\prime}})))[\alpha].

Hence we may conclude that:

\triangleright^{\kappa}{(\alpha:\kappa)}\ ((\zeta^{\kappa}{(}\overline{f}(\mu))[\alpha]){}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}(\overline{g}(\nu))),

which proves that
\overline{f}(\mu){}\,{}\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu)
via the condition
2
of Definition
7.2
.

•

If there exist
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A},\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, and
p:(0,1)
, such that
\mu={\mu_{1}}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{2}}
then
from
\mu{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu
we know that there exist
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{B}
such that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
,
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
, and
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
.

Note that
\overline{f}(\mu)={\overline{f}(\mu_{1})}\oplus_{p}{\overline{f}(\mu_{2})}
. Since
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{D}{A}
, the first case of this lemma proves that from
\mu_{1}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{1}
we may conclude
\overline{f}(\mu_{1}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu_{1})
. Similarly since
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{D}(\triangleright^{\kappa}{}\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{A})
, the second case of this lemma proves that from
\mu_{2}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\nu_{2}
we may conclude
\overline{f}(\mu_{2}){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu_{2})
.

Then by Lemma
7.8
:
\overline{f}(\mu)={\overline{f}(\mu_{1})}\oplus_{p}{\overline{f}(\mu_{2})}{}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}{\overline{g}(\nu_{1})}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\overline{g}(\nu_{2})}=\overline{g}({\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}})
.

Finally, by Lemma
7.5
and the fact that
\nu\leadsto^{\approx}{\nu_{1}}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{2}}
, we conclude
\overline{f}(\mu){}\,{}\overline{{}\,\mathcal{R}\,{}}^{\kappa}\,{}\overline{g}(\nu)
.

∎

Section
8

In preparation for the proofs of Lemmas
8.2
and
8.3
we prove several lemmas which show that
{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}
is compatible with the typing rules in Figure
2
.

Lemma A.3.

Let
f:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\sigma}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\tau}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}
and
g:\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\sigma}}\to\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\tau}}
as well as
\mu:\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\sigma}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}}
and
\nu:\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}{(\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\sigma}})}
such that
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu
.
If for any
v:\sigma
and
V:\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\sigma}}
we have that
(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma}\,{}V)\to(f(v){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau}\,{}g(V))
it follows that

(\mu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ f){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}(\nu>\!>\!=\delta^{\forall\!}\circ g).

or equivalently

\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(f\right)(\mu){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}\left(g\right)(\nu)

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
7.9
.
Looking at the requirements, it suffices to prove that
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma}\,{}V
implies
\delta^{\kappa}{(f(v))}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mathsf{Nat}}\,{}\delta^{\forall\!}{(g(V))}
.
Using Lemma
7.9
, this follows from our assumption that
(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma}\,{}V)\to(f(v){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau}\,{}g(V))
.
∎

Lemma A.4.

Let
f:\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\sigma_{1}\times\sigma_{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\sigma_{3}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}
and
g:\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\sigma_{1}}}\times\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\sigma_{2}}}\to\mathsf{Val}_{\mathsf{\sigma_{3}}}
as well as
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}d
and
\nu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma_{2}}\,{}e
.
If we have that

(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}V)\to(w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{2}}\,{}W)\to(f(v,w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{3}}\,{}g(V,W))

it follows that

\displaystyle(\mu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.\nu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}(f(v,w))){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}

\displaystyle(d>\!>\!=\lambda V.e>\!>\!=\lambda W.\delta^{\forall\!}(g(V,W))).

Proof.

We apply Lemma
7.9
with the functions
\lambda v.\nu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}{(f(v,w))}
and
\lambda V.e>\!>\!=\lambda W.\delta^{\forall\!}(g(V,W))
.
Since we have that
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}d
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}V)\to(

\displaystyle(\nu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}{(f(v,w))}){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma_{3}}\,{}(e>\!>\!=\lambda W.\delta^{\forall\!}(g(V,W))))

Let now
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}V
, using Lemma
A.3
for the functions
\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}{(f(v,w))}
and
\lambda W.\delta^{\forall\!}(g(V,W))
, it suffices to show that

(w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{2}}\,{}W)\to(f(v,w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{3}}\,{}g(V,W))

and the claim follows.
∎

Corollary A.5.

Let
{{\smash{\mathbf{op}}}}\in\{{{\smash{\mathbf{suc}}}},{{\smash{\mathbf{pred}}}}{}\}
and
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mathsf{Nat}}\,{}\nu
. Then

\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{op}}}}\right)(\mu){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mathsf{Nat}}\,{}\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}\left({{\smash{\mathbf{op}}}}\right)(\nu).

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
A.3
. Note that if
n=m
, then also
{{\smash{\mathbf{op}}}}(n)={{\smash{\mathbf{op}}}}(m)
.
∎

The arguments for
{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{}
,
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{}
,
\mathsf{pr}_{1}{}
and
\mathsf{pr}_{2}{}
proceed similarly.

Corollary A.6.

Assume that
\mu_{1}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mathsf{Nat}}\,{}\nu_{1}
,
\mu_{2}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu_{2}
and
\mu_{3}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu_{3}
. Then also

\left(\mu_{1}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}0&\mapsto\mu_{2}\\
n+1&\mapsto\mu_{3}\end{cases}\right){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\left(\nu_{1}>\!>\!=\begin{cases}\underline{0}&\mapsto\nu_{2}\\
\underline{n+1}&\mapsto\nu_{3}\end{cases}\right)

Proof.

We apply the bind lemma for

f=\begin{cases}0&\mapsto\mu_{2}\\
n+1&\mapsto\mu_{3}\end{cases}

and

g=\begin{cases}\underline{0}&\mapsto\nu_{2}\\
\underline{n+1}&\mapsto\nu_{3}\end{cases}
.
It thus suffices to show that for any
u{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mathsf{Nat}}\,{}U
we have that

f(u){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}g(U)
.

We proceed by case distinction on
u:\mathsf{Nat}
.

Case
:
u=0

Then necessarily also
U=\underline{0}
and thus
f(u)=\mu_{2}
and
g(U)=\nu_{2}
.
But
\mu_{2}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu_{2}
is one of our assumptions.

Case
:
u=n+1

This case is analogous to the previous case.

∎

Corollary A.7.

Let
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}d
and
\nu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}e
, then

\displaystyle(\mu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.\nu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}{((v,w))})

\displaystyle{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma}\times{\tau}}\,{}(d>\!>\!=\lambda V.e>\!>\!=\lambda W.\delta^{\forall\!}(\langle{V},{W}\rangle)).

Proof.

Using
A.4
it suffices to show that

(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma}\,{}V)\to(w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau}\,{}W)\to((v,w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\times{\tau}}\,{}(V,W))

which is immediate by the definition of
(v,w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\times{\tau}}\,{}(V,W)
.
∎

Lemma A.8.

Let
\mu_{1}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\nu_{2}
and
\mu_{2}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu_{2}
, then

(\mu_{1}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}v.\mu_{2}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}w.v(w)){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}(\nu_{1}>\!>\!=\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M}).\nu_{2}>\!>\!=\lambda W.\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}(M[W/x]))).

Proof.

To show this, we use Lemma
7.9
on the functions

(1)

f_{1}\triangleq\lambda v.\mu_{2}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.v(w)

(2)

g_{1}\triangleq\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M}).\nu_{2}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}(M[W/x]))

and it remains to show that assuming
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M}
we get that

\left(\mu_{2}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.v(w)\right){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\left(\nu_{2}>\!>\!=\lambda W.\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{(M[W/x])})\right)

We proceed by using Lemma
7.9
once more, this time for the functions

(1)

f_{2}\triangleq\lambda w.v(w)
and

(2)

g_{2}\triangleq\lambda W.\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}(M[W/x]))
.

Now it suffices to prove that assuming
(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M})
and
(w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}W)
we get

v(w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{}(M[W/x]))

Observe that by the definition of
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M}
we immediately get that
v(w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{}(M[W/x])
and thus
the result follows by Lemma
7.5
.
∎

Lemma A.9.

Assume
\mu_{1}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma_{1}}+{\sigma_{2}}}\,{}\nu_{1}
as well as

(1)

\forall v,V.v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}V\to e_{1}(v){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}(M[V/x]))

(2)

\forall v,V.v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{2}}\,{}V\to e_{2}(v){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}(N[V/x]))

then it follows that

\displaystyle\left(\mu_{1}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto e_{1}(v)\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}\mapsto e_{2}(v)\end{cases}\right){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\left(\nu_{1}>\!>\!=\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{(M[V/x])})\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{(N[V/x])})\end{cases}\right)

Proof.

Again, we use Lemma
7.9
and considering the assumptions of the lemma, it only remains to show that given
w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma_{1}}+{\sigma_{2}}}\,{}W
we also have

\displaystyle\left(\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto e_{1}(v)\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}\mapsto e_{2}(v)\end{cases}\right)(w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\left(\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{(M[V/x])})\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{(N[V/x])})\end{cases}\right)(W)

This we can show by a case distinction on
w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma_{1}}+{\sigma_{2}}}\,{}W
.
In the case of
{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{w}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma_{1}}+{\sigma_{2}}}\,{}\mathsf{inl}{\,W}

it remains to show that

e_{1}(w){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\mathsf{eval}{(M[W/x])})

which follows from the assumptions. The other case is similar.
∎

Lemma A.10.

If
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}\nu
then also

\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}\right)(\mu){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}\left(\mathsf{fold}{\,}\right)(\nu).

Proof.

We apply Lemma
A.3
to
f\triangleq\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}
and
g\triangleq\mathsf{fold}{\,}
.
It thus suffices to show that for
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}V
we have that
\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}{v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{fold}{\,V}
.
This is however immediate, since

\displaystyle\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}{v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{fold}{\,V}

\displaystyle\triangleq({\triangleright}\,(\alpha\!:\!\kappa).(\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}{v})\,[\alpha]{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}V)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow{\triangleright}\,(\alpha\!:\!\kappa).(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}V)

∎

Lemma A.11.

If
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\nu
then

\displaystyle(\mu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\lambda(\alpha:\kappa).\delta^{\kappa}{(v[\alpha])}))

\displaystyle{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}(\nu>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda(\mathsf{fold}{\,V}).\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{(\delta^{\forall\!}{(V)})})

Proof.

We apply Lemma
7.9
with the functions
\lambda v.\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\lambda(\alpha:\kappa).v[\alpha])
and
\lambda(\mathsf{fold}{\,V}).\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\delta^{\forall\!}{}(V))
.
It thereby suffices to show that if
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}(\mathsf{fold}{\,V})
then also

\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\lambda(\alpha:\kappa).\delta^{\kappa}{(v[\alpha])}){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\delta^{\forall\!}(V)).

Note that
v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}(\mathsf{fold}{\,V})
is equivalent to
\triangleright\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\alpha:\kappa$}\mathclose{}{)}}.{}(v[\alpha]{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}V)
, which in turn,
by Lemma
7.5
, is equivalent to

\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\lambda(\alpha:\kappa).\delta^{\kappa}{(v[\alpha])}){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{}(\delta^{\forall\!}(V)).

This concludes the claim.
∎

Lemma A.12.

If
\mu{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu
and
\mu_{1}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\nu_{1}
then also

{\mu}\oplus^{\kappa}_{p}{\mu_{1}}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}{\nu}\oplus^{\!\forall\!}_{p}{\nu_{1}}

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
7.8
.
∎

A.0.1.
The Guarded fundamental lemma and congruence lemma

Proof of Lemma
8.3
.

The proof proceeds by induction on
M:\mathsf{Tm}^{\Gamma}_{\mathsf{\sigma}}
and relies almost entirely on the compatibility lemmas with the only exception of the
M=\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}
case.

Case
:
\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}

By induction hypothesis, we have that

\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma,\sigma}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(M^{\prime}[\delta])}.

Thus we get that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \forall\rho,\delta,v,V.&(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta)\wedge(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma}\,{}V)\\
&\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho,v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(M^{\prime}[\delta,V/x])}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} \forall\rho,\delta.&(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta)\to\forall v,V.(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma}\,{}V)\\
&\to(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho,v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((M^{\prime}[\delta])[V/x])})\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}[\delta]}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} \forall\rho,\delta.&(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta)\\
&\to\delta^{\kappa}{(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}_{\rho})}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\delta^{\forall\!}(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}[\delta]})\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\delta^{\forall\!}(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}[\delta])\right)

Case
:
M\,N

Let
\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta
, by induction hypothesis we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma}\to{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(M[\delta])}
and
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\sigma}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(N[\delta])}
.
Now Lemma
A.8
implies that
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle MN$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((M\,N)[\delta])}
.

Case
:
\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.N)

Let
\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta
, by induction hypothesis we have

(1)

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle L$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\sigma_{1}}+{\sigma_{2}}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(L[\delta])}

(2)

\forall v,V.(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{1}}\,{}V)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(M[\delta,V/x])}

(3)

\forall w,W.(w{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\sigma_{2}}\,{}W)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.w}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(N[\delta,V/y])}
.

By Lemma
A.9
we then get

\displaystyle\left(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle L$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{w}\mapsto\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.w}\end{cases}\right){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\left(\mathsf{eval}{(L[\delta])}>\!>\!=\begin{cases}\mathsf{inl}{\,V}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{(\mathsf{eval}{(M[\delta][V/x])})}\\
\mathsf{inr}{\,V}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\!\forall\!}{(\mathsf{eval}{(N[\delta][V/x])})}\end{cases}\right)

Observe, that this is precisely

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.M)$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.M)[\delta])}

Case
:
\mathsf{unfold}{\,M}

Let
\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Gamma}\,{}\delta
, by the induction hypothesis we have
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(M[\delta])}
and now
Lemma
A.11
implies that

\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{unfold}{\,M}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(\mathsf{unfold}{\,M}[\delta])}

The remaining cases follow similarly.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.2
.

The proof proceeds by induction on context derivations. All cases — except for the
M=\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}
case — are direct consequences of the compatibility lemmas.

Case
:
C=\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{C^{\prime}}:({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta}\vdash{\tau_{1}\to\tau_{2}})

Assume
M\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\sigma}N
. We have that
C^{\prime}:({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta,(x:\tau_{1})}\vdash{\tau_{2}})
and by the induction hypothesis it follows that

C^{\prime}[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\tau_{2}}C^{\prime}[N]

It now follows that

\displaystyle C^{\prime}[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\tau_{2}}C^{\prime}[N]\triangleq\

\displaystyle\forall\rho,\delta.\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta,\tau_{1}}\,{}\delta\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau_{2}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((C^{\prime}[N])[\delta])}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \forall\rho,\delta,v,V.&\left(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta\wedge v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau_{1}}\,{}V\right)\\
&\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau_{2}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((C^{\prime}[N])[\delta,V/x])}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \forall&\rho,\delta(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\\
&\to\left(\begin{aligned} \forall&v,V.v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau_{1}}\,{}V\\
&\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau_{2}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((C^{\prime}[N])[\delta,V/x])}\end{aligned}\right)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \forall&\rho,\delta(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\\
&\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{C^{\prime}[M]}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{{\tau_{1}}\to{\tau_{2}}}\,{}(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{C^{\prime}[N]})[\delta,V/x]\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \forall&\rho,\delta(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\\
&\to\delta^{\kappa}{(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{C^{\prime}[M]}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}_{\rho})}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\tau_{1}}\to{\tau_{2}}}\,{}\delta^{\forall\!}{(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{C^{\prime}[N]})[\delta,V/x]}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left(\forall\rho,\delta(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\tau_{1}}\to{\tau_{2}}}\,{}(C[N])[\delta,V/x]\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle C[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{{\tau_{1}}\to{\tau_{2}}}C[N]

Case
:
C=\mathsf{fold}{\,C^{\prime}}:({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta}\vdash{\mu{X}.{\tau}})

Assume
M\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\sigma}N
. We have that
C^{\prime}:({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta}\vdash{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]})
and thus by induction hypothesis we have

C^{\prime}[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}C^{\prime}[N].

By Lemma
A.10
we have that

\displaystyle C^{\prime}[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}C^{\prime}[N]

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\left(\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau[\mu{X}.{\tau}/X]}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((C^{\prime}[N])[\delta])}\right)

\displaystyle\to\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\\
&\to\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\mathsf{next}^{\kappa}\right)(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho})\right){}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{D}^{\!\forall\!}\left(\mathsf{fold}{\,}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}{}\left({C^{\prime}[N]}[\delta]\right)\right)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{fold}{\,C^{\prime}[M]}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((\mathsf{fold}{\,C^{\prime}[N]})[\delta])}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle C[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\mu{X}.{\tau}}C[N]

Case
:
C=\mathsf{case}(L,x.C^{\prime},y.N):({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta}\vdash{\tau})

Assume that
M\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\sigma}M^{\prime}
. We furthermore have that

(1)

\Delta\vdash L:{\tau_{1}}+{\tau_{2}}

(2)

C^{\prime}:({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta,x:\tau_{1}}\vdash{\tau})

(3)

\Delta,y:\tau_{2}\vdash N:\tau

By the induction hypothesis and Lemma
8.3
it follows directly that

C^{\prime}[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\tau}C^{\prime}[M^{\prime}]
,
L\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{{\tau_{1}}+{\tau_{2}}}L
and
N\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\tau}N
.
Thus, for all
\rho
and
\delta
such that
\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta
, we get that

\displaystyle(1)\quad\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle L$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{{\tau_{1}}+{\tau_{2}}}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(L[\delta])}

\displaystyle(2)\quad\begin{aligned} \forall v,V.&(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau_{1}}\,{}V)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{((C^{\prime}[M^{\prime}])[\delta,V/x])}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle(3)\quad\forall v,V.(v{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\tau_{2}}\,{}V)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(N[\delta,V/x])}

Now, using Lemma
A.9
we conclude that

\displaystyle\left(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle L$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}{{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}}\mapsto{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C^{\prime}[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}}\\
{{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}}\mapsto{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.v}}\end{cases}\right)

\displaystyle{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(\mathsf{case}(L,x.C^{\prime}[M^{\prime}],y.N)[\delta])}

and thus we get that

\forall\rho,\delta.(\rho{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Val}}_{\Delta}\,{}\delta)\to\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle C[M]$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}{}\,\preceq^{\kappa,{\sf Tm}}_{\tau}\,{}\mathsf{eval}{(C[M^{\prime}])}

Case
:
C=\mathsf{choice}^{p}(C^{\prime},N):({\Gamma}\vdash{\sigma})\Rightarrow({\Delta}\vdash{\tau})

Assume
M\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\sigma}M^{\prime}
. It follows from the assumptions that furthermore
\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
and thus by
Lemma
8.3
we get
N\preceq^{\kappa,\Gamma}_{\sigma}N
.
Now the induction hypothesis implies
C^{\prime}[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Delta}_{\sigma}C^{\prime}[M^{\prime}]
and consequently
C[M]\preceq^{\kappa,\Delta}_{\tau}C[M^{\prime}]
follows by Lemma
A.12
.

The remaining cases are similar.
∎

A.0.2.
Proof of Theorem
6.1

The proof of Theorem
6.1
is only briefly sketched in the main text. We start by
defining the alternative denotational semantics
\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis
and proving it sound with respect to the operational semantics.
First recall that
\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis
agrees with
\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
on types and most terms. The only place where they do not agree are

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle MN$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\triangleq\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda f.\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.fv

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{MN}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\triangleq\llparenthesis{M}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda f.\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\char 12\relax\alpha\kappa fv)

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.N)$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\triangleq\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle L$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.x\mapsto v}}\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}\mapsto{\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle N$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho.y\mapsto v}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.N)}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho}

\displaystyle\triangleq\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\SavedStyle L$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}^{\kappa}_{\rho}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}{{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}{}(\char 12\relax\alpha\kappa{\llparenthesis{M}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho.x\mapsto v}})\\
{{\smash{\mathbf{inr}}}}\,{v}\mapsto\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}{}(\char 12\relax\alpha\kappa{\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho.y\mapsto v}})\end{cases}

We first show the following soundness theorem.

Theorem A.13 (Soundness).

For any well typed closed expression
\cdot\vdash M:\sigma
we have that

\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M)=\llparenthesis{M}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

Proof.

First, assume the guarded hypothesis

\forall M:\mathsf{Tm}_{\mathsf{\sigma}}.(\triangleright\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle\alpha:\kappa$}\mathclose{}{)}}.{(}\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})=\llparenthesis{M}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}).

We proceed with case analysis of term derivations.

Case
:
M=V

The value cases are all the same:

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}V)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\delta^{\kappa}(V))

\displaystyle=\delta^{\kappa}(\llparenthesis{V}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})

\displaystyle=\llparenthesis{V}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

Case
:
M=\mathsf{suc}{\,M^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(\mathsf{suc}{\,M^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{suc}}}}{}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{{{\smash{\mathbf{suc}}}}(-)}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{suc}}}}(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{suc}}}}{}\right)\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{suc}}}}{}\right)\left(\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle=\llparenthesis{\mathsf{suc}{\,M^{\prime}}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

The case of
M=\mathsf{pred}\,{M^{\prime}}
is similar

Case
:
M=\mathsf{ifz}(L,M^{\prime},N)

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(\mathsf{ifz}(L,M^{\prime},N)))=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}\underline{0}\mapsto\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M^{\prime})\\
\underline{n+1}\mapsto\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(N)\end{cases}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}\underline{0}\mapsto\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M^{\prime}))\\
\underline{n+1}\mapsto\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(N))\end{cases}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}\underline{0}\mapsto\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\\
\underline{n+1}\mapsto\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda\underline{n}.\mathsf{match\ }\llparenthesis{\underline{n}}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\mathsf{\ with}\begin{cases}0\mapsto\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\\
n+1\mapsto\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}0\mapsto\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\\
n+1\mapsto\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{\mathsf{ifz}(L,M^{\prime},N)}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

Case
:
M=\langle{M^{\prime}},{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{\langle{M^{\prime}},{N}\rangle})=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.\delta^{\kappa}(\langle{V},{W}\rangle))\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\delta^{\kappa}(\langle{V},{W}\rangle)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.\delta^{\kappa}(\llparenthesis{\langle{V},{W}\rangle}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.\delta^{\kappa}(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{\llparenthesis{{V}}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}},{\llparenthesis{{W}}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}}$}\mathclose{}{)}})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}))>\!>\!=^{\kappa}&\lambda v.(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N))\\
&>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{v},{w}$}\mathclose{}{)}})\end{aligned}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\delta^{\kappa}(\ThisStyle{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\SavedStyle{v},{w}$}\mathclose{}{)}})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{\langle{M^{\prime}},{N}\rangle}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

Case
:
M=\mathsf{fst}{\,M^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{(\mathsf{fst}{\,M^{\prime}})})=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\underline{\pi_{1}}\right){}(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}})\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{\underline{\pi_{1}}{(-)}}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\mathsf{pr}_{1}{}\right){}\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\mathsf{pr}_{1}{}\right)\left(\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{\mathsf{fst}{\,M^{\prime}}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

The case of
M=\mathsf{snd}{\,M^{\prime}}
is similar.

Case
:
M=MN

We start by unfolding the definition of
(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}MN)>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}
and use associativity as well as the definition of
>\!>\!=^{\kappa}
to get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M&>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}).\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\\
&\lambda V.(\Delta^{\kappa}(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M^{\prime}[V/x])))\big)>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}&\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}).\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N\\
&>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.(\Delta^{\kappa}\big(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M^{\prime}[V/x])\big)>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}&\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}).\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N\\
&>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.\Delta^{\kappa}\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M^{\prime}[V/x])>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\delta^{\kappa}\circ\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}&\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}).\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N\\
&>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.(\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}{}(\lambda\alpha.\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}[V/x]}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\rho}))\end{aligned}\right)

In the last step we applied the guarded hypothesis.
Next, we use the substitution lemma for terms and recall the definition of semantic function type values, which leaves us with

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}).\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.(\Delta^{\kappa}(\llparenthesis{\ M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\llparenthesis{W}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}}))

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} \mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}&\lambda(\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}).\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N\\
&>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.(\Delta^{\kappa}(\llparenthesis{\mathsf{lam}\ {x}.{M^{\prime}}}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}(\llparenthesis{W}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})))\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda V.\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda W.(\Delta^{\kappa}(\llparenthesis{V}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}(\llparenthesis{W}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})))

In the last equation, we simply omitted the superfluous case analysis of function values.
We now use the functoriality of
\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}{}
to get that the above equals

\displaystyle(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}M>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})>\!>\!=^{\kappa}

\displaystyle\lambda v.(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}N>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa})>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\Delta^{\kappa}(vw)

which by the induction hypothesis gives

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{M}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda v.\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}_{\cdot}>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\lambda w.\mathsf{step}^{\kappa}(\lambda(\alpha:\kappa).vw)

\displaystyle=\llparenthesis{M}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\cdot\llparenthesis{N}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}=\llparenthesis{MN}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

Case
:
M=\mathsf{inl}{\,M^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{(\mathsf{inl}{\,M^{\prime}})}\right)=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\mathsf{inl}{\,}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{\mathsf{inl}{\,(-)}}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{}\right){}\left(\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right){}\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{M^{\prime}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left({{\smash{\mathbf{inl}}}}\,{}\right){}\left(\llparenthesis{M^{\prime}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\llparenthesis{\mathsf{inl}{\,M^{\prime}}}\rrparenthesis^{\kappa}

The case of
M=\mathsf{inr}{\,M^{\prime}}
is similar.

Case
:
M=\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.N)

We proceed similarly to the function application case and first unfold the definition.

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}{(\mathsf{case}(L,x.M,y.N))}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}\mathsf{inl}{\,V}\mapsto(\Delta^{\kappa}\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M[V/x]))\\
\mathsf{inr}{\,V}\mapsto(\Delta^{\kappa}\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(N[V/x]))\end{cases}\!\!\!\!\!\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}L>\!>\!=^{\kappa}\begin{cases}\mathsf{inl}{\,V}\mapsto\mathsf{D}^{\kappa}\!\!\left(\llparenthesis{-}\rrparenthesis^{\mathsf{Val},\kappa}\right)\left(\Delta^{\kappa}(\mathsf{eval}^{\kappa}(M[V/x]))\right)\\
